href=\"#next\"\u003e\u003cspan\u003e§13\u003c/span\u003eNext steps\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n  \u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003c/nav\u003e\n\n\u003cmain\u003e\n\u003cheader class=\"top\"\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"eyebrow\"\u003eProject review for supervisor · BSc research, index 28381\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003ch1\u003eALMA-GraphRAG: feasibility-aware hotel retrieval over a live knowledge graph\u003c/h1\u003e\n  \u003cp class=\"lede\"\u003eA graph-backed recommender for Colombo hotels that ranks by five feasibility dimensions instead of a single sort key, evaluated against nine baselines. The weights were also tested against a discrete-choice study with 241 analysed participants.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"meta\"\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eDilshan Sanjeewa Deshapriya\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eSupervisor: Dr Rasika Ranaweera\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eSnapshot: 15 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eBranch: sanjeewa/add-data-gather-machanisume\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/header\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §0 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"summary\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§0\u003c/span\u003eThe project in five minutes\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eHotel search normally filters on price or rating and sorts by one attribute. ALMA-GraphRAG stores Colombo hotels in a Neo4j property graph, together with their travel-time edges, traffic signals, news events and amenities. A natural-language question becomes a structured intent. The retriever first removes hotels that fail a hard constraint, then ranks the rest by a weighted sum of five percentile-normalised components: \u003cem\u003espatial, accessibility, facility, economic\u003c/em\u003e and \u003cem\u003edisruption\u003c/em\u003e. Every result carries its per-component breakdown, and an LLM loop (corrective RAG) turns the ranked list into an answer.\u003c/p\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eEvery number on this page comes from the canonical result files regenerated on 14 September 2026. The test suite passes \u003cspan class=\"mono\"\u003e364/364\u003c/span\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"stats\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e0.828\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003enDCG@10, WeightedGraphRAG, 60 queries\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e+0.252\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003eover Filter (OTA-style search), 95% CI [0.175, 0.328], p\u003csub\u003eHolm\u003c/sub\u003e \u0026lt; 0.001\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e−0.248\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003enDCG lost when the feasibility filter is removed: the main driver of the gain\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e0 of 5\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003eweight dimensions identified from wave-1 human choices once position bias is controlled\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"verdict\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n      \u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan class=\"dot\" style=\"background:var(--good)\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003eHolds up\u003c/h4\u003e\n      \u003cul\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eBeats all seven non-circular baselines, significant after Holm correction\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eWins at all six gold-tolerance scales\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eAll 1,000 random weight vectors beat Filter, so the result doesn\u0027t depend on a lucky weight choice\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eRankings are explainable: every score decomposes into components\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003ePersonalisation demo: same F1 event, disjoint top-5 lists (0 overlap)\u003c/li\u003e\n      \u003c/ul\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n      \u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan class=\"dot\" style=\"background:var(--caveat)\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003eHolds with caveats\u003c/h4\u003e\n      \u003cul\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eGold is rule-based (graded, tolerance bands), not human-labelled\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eOne city, 58 hotels: a case study, not a benchmark\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eAn LTR model given the gold\u0027s own predicates scores 0.962, which shows how learnable the rule gold is\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003e41% of hotels have no price\u003c/li\u003e\n      \u003c/ul\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n      \u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan class=\"dot\" style=\"background:var(--bad)\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003eNot supported yet\u003c/h4\u003e\n      \u003cul\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003e\"Disruption-aware\": removing disruption or diffusion changes 0 of 60 top-10 sets\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003e\"Weights learned from humans\": wave-1 fails placebo and position-control gates\u003c/li\u003e\n \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §1 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"questions\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§1\u003c/span\u003eProblem and research questions\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eA traveller\u0027s real question is not \"which hotel is cheapest\" but \"which hotel can I realistically get to, given where I need to be, what is happening in the city, and what I care about\". The proposition is to treat that as \u003cstrong\u003efeasibility-first, multi-criteria retrieval over a graph whose edges carry live, expiring signals\u003c/strong\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n \u003c/table\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §2 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"architecture\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§2\u003c/span\u003eEnd-to-end architecture\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eFour external providers feed scheduled ingestion jobs, which write to Neo4j with idempotent \u003ccode\u003eMERGE\u003c/code\u003e statements. Hotel text is mirrored into pgvector for the lexical and dense baselines. At query time, the parser, retriever and corrective-RAG loop read the graph. A separate Next.js study app collects human choices; Python fitters turn those choices into candidate weight profiles.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nflowchart LR\n  subgraph SRC[\"External providers\"]\n    GP[\"Google Places\"]\n    LA[\"LiteAPI rates, rooms, facilities\"]\n    NW[\"NewsAPI, GNews, RSS\"]\n    GD[\"Google Distance Matrix\"]\n    TT[\"TomTom flow and incidents\"]\n  end\n  subgraph ING[\"Ingestion - query\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nsequenceDiagram\n  autonumber\n  actor U as Traveller\n  participant API as FastAPI /query\n  participant RC as Redis\n  participant QP as query_parser\n  participant WR as WeightedRetriever\n  participant NEO as Neo4j\n  participant LLM as LLM\n  U-\u003e\u003eAPI: question, city\n  API-\u003e\u003eRC: get md5(city, question)\n  alt cache hit\n    RC--\u003e\u003eAPI: cached answer\n    API--\u003e\u003eU: answer\n  else cache miss\n    API-\u003e\u003eQP: parse_query\n    QP-\u003e\u003eQP: regex slots (deterministic)\n    QP-\u003e\u003eLLM: slot-fill proximity, sort, price direction\n    LLM--\u003e\u003eQP: slots (neutral values never override regex)\n    QP--\u003e\u003eWR: QueryIntent\n    WR-\u003e\u003eNEO: candidate query with 2-hop NEAR_HOTEL diffusion\n    NEO--\u003e\u003eWR: 58 candidates + collected attributes\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: base profile + intent ladder, renormalise\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: feasibility filter (unknown value fails)\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: percentile components, weighted sum, top 10\n    WR--\u003e\u003eAPI: ranked hotels + breakdown\n    API-\u003e\u003eLLM: grade relevance of context\n    opt score below 0.6 and retries left\n      API-\u003e\u003eLLM: rewrite question\n      API-\u003e\u003eWR: retrieve again\n      API-\u003e\u003eLLM: re-grade\n    end\n    API-\u003e\u003eLLM: generate grounded answer\n    API-\u003e\u003eRC: set, TTL 900 s\n    API--\u003e\u003eU: answer + ranked hotels\n  end\n\u003c/pre\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cfigcaption\u003e\u003cb\u003eFigure 5.1.\u003c/b\u003e Request path for \u003ccode\u003ePOST /query\u003c/code\u003e. The LLM never writes Cypher; it only fills a fixed \u003ccode\u003eQueryIntent\u003c/code\u003e schema that binds to a parameterised query. This keeps the data path injection-safe. For reproducibility, the evaluation uses frozen intent caches.\u003c/figcaption\u003e\n  \u003c/figure\u003e\n\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n      \u003csvg measured. The accuracy gain over OTA-style search comes mostly from the filter that runs \u003cem\u003ebefore\u003c/em\u003e scoring. The disruption term is drawn dashed because it has no measurable effect on the current data snapshot.\u003c/figcaption\u003e\n  \u003c/figure\u003e\n\n  \u003ch3\u003eScoring model\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"formula\"\u003escore(h) = w_spa·spatial + w_acc·accessibility + w_fac·facility + w_eco·economic + w_dis·disruption    Σw = 1\nspatial       = percentile, lower distance_km better          (flipped if proximity = far)\naccessibility = percentile, lower travel_time_traffic_min better\nfacility      = 0.40·amenity match + 0.20·attraction match + 0.20·pct(rating) + 0.20·pct(star)\neconomic      = percentile, lower price better; unknown price → 0.5   (flipped if price = high)\ndisruption    = own route signal (0.7) blended with NEAR_HOTEL neighbourhood exposure (0.3)\u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"tablewrap\"\u003e\n      \u003ctable\u003e\n Three results reversed along the way, and this timeline records them rather than smoothing them over.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003col class=\"timeline\"\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJun 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eWeighted retriever, personalisation, first harness\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eReplaced a single-MATCH context dump with intent parsing and composite scoring. Cleaned city aliases, duplicate hotels (49 → 40) and placeholder prices.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJul 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eBenchmark hardening\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eFound the gold was circular: Filter was graded by its own predicate. Rebuilt it as graded 2/1/0 with tolerance bands, where any hard failure disqualifies. Added pgvector keyword, dense and hybrid baselines, plus paired bootstrap, Wilcoxon and Holm tests. Built a balanced set of 60 queries, 10 per category.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJul–Aug 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eDiscrete-choice study, wave 1\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eNext.js app with real LiteAPI hotels and rooms: 11 tasks, 32 hotels, a hotel stage then a room stage. 268 consented and 241 were analysed, giving 2,232 hotel choices.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli class=\"neg\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e1 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eRL weight policy: null\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eA Dirichlet REINFORCE policy under nested CV scored 0.7991 ± 0.0074 against 0.8016 for the hand-tuned rules. An earlier positive result came from model selection on the test fold.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e2 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eScale fix and price slice\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eServing switched to percentile components. Added a price-direction slot and a 14-query ranked price slice, so the economic weight is observable at all.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli class=\"neg\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e13 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eWeight audit: wave 1 does not identify weights\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003e99.4% of choices were in the top 3 positions. With correct position controls, the components add nothing (χ²₅ = 8.7, p = .12). Wave 2 was redesigned with randomised order and gated identification.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e14 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eFull re-run, robustness, journal draft\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eFrozen intents for bit-identical runs. Added ablations, a 1,000-vector weight simplex, tolerance sensitivity, latency and a deployment audit. to read the profile rows\u003c/h4\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eOn the main set, no profile differs significantly from handset after Holm correction. The main set only separates profiles on amenity, accessibility and disruption queries (30 of 60 top-10 sets change). The human route has 95 clean-cohort participants, 28 held out with 280 choices, and it favours location-heavy profiles. But those participants chose from lists \u003cem\u003esorted by the same components\u003c/em\u003e, so part of that agreement is top-of-list clicking, not preference (§11).\u003c/p\u003e\n \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"callout key\"\u003e\n    \u003ch4\u003eWhat the robustness evidence says\u003c/h4\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eThe contribution is \u003cstrong\u003efeasibility-first retrieval over graph-held travel-time features\u003c/strong\u003e: a hard-constraint gate followed by any reasonable blend of percentile components. It is not a particular weight vector, and on this snapshot it is not live disruption. The disruption signal cannot matter yet: pool disruption scores range 0.849–1.000 (sd 0.047), the largest route delay is 0.2 min, and no query is graded on delay.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §11 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"weights\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§11\u003c/span\u003eWeight-elicitation study\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eThe aim was to replace hand-set weights with weights estimated from human booking choices, using a discrete-choice experiment and a conditional logit model. Wave 1 collected 2,232 hotel choices from 241 analysed participants across 11 persona tasks (for example, \"Evening walker, Galle Face\"). A second stage, choosing a room within the chosen hotel, estimated willingness to pay.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"tablewrap\"\u003e\n \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"callout caveat\"\u003e\n    \u003ch4\u003eFinding: wave 1 measures list position, not preference\u003c/h4\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eParticipants picked from the top three of a list sorted by one of the very components being estimated, and 99.9% opened only one hotel. A −log(rank) control predicts a long tail that does not exist, so the components absorb position and look significant. With rank dummies, adding components makes held-out perplexity slightly \u003cem\u003eworse\u003c/em\u003e (3.024 → 3.027). The signal that does exist is in the \u003cstrong\u003esort choice\u003c/strong\u003e: 86.8% used the sort control, and on the cost-first task t3, 51% of those who switched chose price low-to-high. Stage 2 is valid within a hotel (β\u003csub\u003eprice\u003c/sub\u003e −1.976 per 10,000 LKR; breakfast worth ≈ 2,989 LKR), but it cannot be bridged to hotel-level weights, because the nested-logit λ = −0.036 falls outside (0, 1].\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003ch3\u003eWave 2: implemented, not yet fielded\u003c/h3\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv href=\"#robust\"\u003e\u003cspan\u003e§10\u003c/span\u003eRobustness\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003ca href=\"#weights\"\u003e\u003cspan\u003e§11\u003c/span\u003eWeight study\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003ca href=\"#status\"\u003e\u003cspan\u003e§12\u003c/span\u003eHonest status\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003ca href=\"#next\"\u003e\u003cspan\u003e§13\u003c/span\u003eNext steps\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n  \u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003c/nav\u003e\n\n\u003cmain\u003e\n\u003cheader class=\"top\"\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"eyebrow\"\u003eProject review for supervisor · BSc research, index 28381\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003ch1\u003eALMA-GraphRAG: feasibility-aware hotel retrieval over a live knowledge graph\u003c/h1\u003e\n  \u003cp class=\"lede\"\u003eA graph-backed recommender for Colombo hotels that ranks by five feasibility dimensions instead of a single sort key, evaluated against nine baselines. The weights were also tested against a discrete-choice study with 241 analysed participants.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"meta\"\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eDilshan Sanjeewa Deshapriya\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eSupervisor: Dr Rasika Ranaweera\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eSnapshot: 15 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eBranch: sanjeewa/add-data-gather-machanisume\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/header\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §0 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"summary\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§0\u003c/span\u003eThe project in five minutes\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eHotel search normally filters on price or rating and sorts by one attribute. ALMA-GraphRAG stores Colombo hotels in a Neo4j property graph, together with their travel-time edges, traffic signals, news events and amenities. A natural-language question becomes a structured intent. The retriever first removes hotels that fail a hard constraint, then ranks the rest by a weighted sum of five percentile-normalised components: \u003cem\u003espatial, accessibility, facility, economic\u003c/em\u003e and \u003cem\u003edisruption\u003c/em\u003e. Every result carries its per-component breakdown, and an LLM loop (corrective RAG) turns the ranked list into an answer.\u003c/p\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eEvery number on this page comes from the canonical result files regenerated on 14 September 2026. The test suite passes \u003cspan class=\"mono\"\u003e364/364\u003c/span\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"stats\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e0.828\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003enDCG@10, WeightedGraphRAG, 60 queries\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e+0.252\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003eover Filter (OTA-style search), 95% CI [0.175, 0.328], p\u003csub\u003eHolm\u003c/sub\u003e \u0026lt; 0.001\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e−0.248\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003enDCG lost when the feasibility filter is removed: the main driver of the gain\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e2,232\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003ehuman choices used to estimate weight profiles; the 13 Sep audit found list position, not preference, drives them\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"verdict\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n      \u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan class=\"dot\" correction\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eWins at all six gold-tolerance scales\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eAll 1,000 random weight vectors beat Filter, so the result doesn\u0027t depend on a lucky weight choice\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eRankings are explainable: every score decomposes into components\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003ePersonalisation demo: same F1 event, disjoint top-5 lists (0 overlap)\u003c/li\u003e\n      \u003c/ul\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n      \u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan class=\"dot\" style=\"background:var(--caveat)\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003eHolds with caveats\u003c/h4\u003e\n      \u003cul\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eGold is rule-based (graded, tolerance bands), not human-labelled\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eOne city, 58 hotels: a case study, not a benchmark\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eAn LTR model given the gold\u0027s own predicates scores 0.962, which shows how learnable the rule gold is\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003e41% of hotels have no price\u003c/li\u003e\n      \u003c/ul\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n removing disruption or diffusion changes 0 of 60 top-10 sets\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003e\"Weights learned from humans\": wave-1 fails placebo and position-control gates\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eRL weight policy: trained, null result\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eCorrective-RAG answer quality: never measured\u003c/li\u003e\n      \u003c/ul\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §1 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"questions\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§1\u003c/span\u003eProblem and research questions\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eA traveller\u0027s real question is not \"which hotel is cheapest\" but \"which hotel can I realistically get to, given where I need to be, what is happening in the city, and what I care about\". The proposition is to treat that as \u003cstrong\u003efeasibility-first, multi-criteria retrieval over a graph whose edges carry live, expiring signals\u003c/strong\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n \u003c/table\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §2 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"architecture\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§2\u003c/span\u003eEnd-to-end architecture\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eFour external providers feed scheduled ingestion jobs, which write to Neo4j with idempotent \u003ccode\u003eMERGE\u003c/code\u003e statements. Hotel text is mirrored into pgvector for the lexical and dense baselines. At query time, the parser, retriever and corrective-RAG loop read the graph. A separate Next.js study app collects human choices; Python fitters turn those choices into candidate weight profiles.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nflowchart LR\n  subgraph SRC[\"External providers\"]\n    GP[\"Google Places\"]\n    LA[\"LiteAPI rates, rooms, facilities\"]\n    NW[\"NewsAPI, GNews, RSS\"]\n    GD[\"Google Distance Matrix\"]\n    TT[\"TomTom flow and incidents\"]\n  end\n  subgraph ING[\"Ingestion - APScheduler\"]\n    HI[\"hotel_ingest + NER extractor\"]\n    NI[\"news ingest + event_linker\"]\n    TI[\"traffic + traffic_linker\"]\n    TOPO[\"build_graph_topology (offline)\"]\n  end\n  subgraph STO[\"Storage - Docker\"]\n    NEO[(\"Neo4j 5.18 property graph\")]\n    PG[(\"pgvector pg16 text + embeddings\")]\n    RD[(\"Redis answer cache, TTL 900 s\")]\n  end\n  subgraph RUN[\"Retrieval and generation\"]\n    QP[\"query_parser: regex + LLM slot-fill\"]\n    WR[\"WeightedRetriever: filter, score, rank\"]\n    CR[\"run_crag: grade, rewrite, generate\"]\n  end\n  subgraph API[\"FastAPI + browser consoles\"]\n    EP[\"/query, /retrieve/debug, /recommend/personalized\"]\n    EV[\"/eval/* walkthrough\"]\n  end\n  subgraph STUDY[\"Weight-elicitation study\"]\n    APP[\"Next.js app, Vercel + Neon\"]\n    FIT[\"fitters, placebo, gates\"]\n  end\n  subgraph EVAL[\"Evaluation harness\"]\n    H1[\"Route 1: rule gold, 60 + 14 queries\"]\n    H2[\"Route 2: human choices\"]\n  end\n  GP --\u003e|every 24 h| HI\n  LA --\u003e|every 24 h| HI\n  NW --\u003e|every 15 min| NI\n  GD --\u003e|every 30 min| TI\n  TT --\u003e|every 30 min| TI\n  HI --\u003e|MERGE Hotel| NEO\n  NI --\u003e|MERGE Event| NEO\n  TI --\u003e|travel time, signals| NEO\n  TOPO --\u003e|NEAR_HOTEL, graded AFFECTED_BY| NEO\n  NEO --\u003e|build_vector_index| PG\n  EP --\u003e CR\n  CR --\u003e QP\n  QP --\u003e|QueryIntent| WR\n  WR --\u003e|one parameterised Cypher| NEO\n  CR --\u003e|cache| RD\n  APP --\u003e|responses| FIT\n  FIT --\u003e|weight profiles| WR\n  EV --\u003e H1\n  EV --\u003e H2\n  H1 --\u003e WR\n  H1 --\u003e PG\n  H2 --\u003e WR\n\u003c/pre\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cfigcaption\u003e\u003cb\u003eFigure 2.1.\u003c/b\u003e System context. query\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nsequenceDiagram\n  autonumber\n  actor U as Traveller\n  participant API as FastAPI /query\n  participant RC as Redis\n  participant QP as query_parser\n  participant WR as WeightedRetriever\n  participant NEO as Neo4j\n  participant LLM as LLM\n  U-\u003e\u003eAPI: question, city\n  API-\u003e\u003eRC: get md5(city, question)\n  alt cache hit\n    RC--\u003e\u003eAPI: cached answer\n    API--\u003e\u003eU: answer\n  else cache miss\n    API-\u003e\u003eQP: parse_query\n    QP-\u003e\u003eQP: regex slots (deterministic)\n    QP-\u003e\u003eLLM: slot-fill proximity, sort, price direction\n    LLM--\u003e\u003eQP: slots (neutral values never override regex)\n    QP--\u003e\u003eWR: QueryIntent\n    WR-\u003e\u003eNEO: candidate query with 2-hop NEAR_HOTEL diffusion\n    NEO--\u003e\u003eWR: 58 candidates + collected attributes\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: base profile + intent ladder, renormalise\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: feasibility filter (unknown value fails)\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: percentile components, weighted sum, top 10\n    WR--\u003e\u003eAPI: ranked hotels + breakdown\n    API-\u003e\u003eLLM: grade relevance of context\n    opt score below 0.6 and retries left\n      API-\u003e\u003eLLM: rewrite question\n      API-\u003e\u003eWR: retrieve again\n      API-\u003e\u003eLLM: re-grade\n    end\n    API-\u003e\u003eLLM: generate grounded answer\n    API-\u003e\u003eRC: set, TTL 900 s\n    API--\u003e\u003eU: answer + ranked hotels\n  end\n\u003c/pre\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cfigcaption\u003e\u003cb\u003eFigure 5.1.\u003c/b\u003e Request path for \u003ccode\u003ePOST /query\u003c/code\u003e. The LLM never writes Cypher; it only fills a fixed \u003ccode\u003eQueryIntent\u003c/code\u003e schema that binds to a parameterised query. This keeps the data path injection-safe. measured. The accuracy gain over OTA-style search comes mostly from the filter that runs \u003cem\u003ebefore\u003c/em\u003e scoring. The disruption term is drawn dashed because it has no measurable effect on the current data snapshot.\u003c/figcaption\u003e\n  \u003c/figure\u003e\n\n  \u003ch3\u003eScoring model\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"formula\"\u003escore(h) = w_spa·spatial + w_acc·accessibility + w_fac·facility + w_eco·economic + w_dis·disruption    Σw = 1\nspatial       = percentile, lower distance_km better          (flipped if proximity = far)\naccessibility = percentile, lower travel_time_traffic_min better\nfacility      = 0.40·amenity match + 0.20·attraction match + 0.20·pct(rating) + 0.20·pct(star)\neconomic      = percentile, lower price better; unknown price → 0.5   (flipped if price = high)\ndisruption    = own route signal (0.7) blended with NEAR_HOTEL neighbourhood exposure (0.3)\u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"tablewrap\"\u003e\n      \u003ctable\u003e\n \u003cstrong\u003ehandset\u003c/strong\u003e is a hand-tuned prior. \u003cstrong\u003eelicited\u003c/strong\u003e, \u003cstrong\u003eblended\u003c/strong\u003e and \u003cstrong\u003ehuman\u003c/strong\u003e came from wave-1 choice-model fits, which the §11 audit does not support. \u003cstrong\u003ebalanced\u003c/strong\u003e was tuned on the evaluation sets, so its scores there are optimistic.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §6 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"states\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§6\u003c/span\u003eState diagrams\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\u003cp\u003eFive state machines govern runtime behaviour: one for each request, signal, background job, user and study participant.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nstateDiagram-v2\n  [*] --\u003e CacheLookup\n  CacheLookup --\u003e Answered: hit\n  CacheLookup --\u003e Retrieve: miss\n  Retrieve --\u003e Grade: context built\n  Retrieve --\u003e RewriteEmpty: empty context\n  RewriteEmpty --\u003e Retrieve: one rewrite\n  Retrieve --\u003e LegacyDump: weighted path failed\n  LegacyDump --\u003e Grade\n  Grade --\u003e Generate: score at least 0.6\n  Grade --\u003e Rewrite: score below 0.6, retries left\n  Grade --\u003e Generate: retries exhausted\n  Rewrite --\u003e Retrieve: reformulated question\n  Generate --\u003e CacheStore\n  CacheStore --\u003e Answered\n  Answered --\u003e [*]\n  note right of Grade\n    CRAG_MIN_SCORE 0.6\n    CRAG_MAX_RETRIES 1\n    LLM unavailable gives 0.5\n  end note\n\u003c/pre\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cfigcaption\u003e\u003cb\u003eFigure 6.1.\u003c/b\u003e Corrective-RAG loop for \u003ccode\u003erun_crag\u003c/code\u003e. This loop is built but not evaluated. Fallback answers are also cached, and the cache key ignores the user profile. Both are deployment issues.\u003c/figcaption\u003e\n  \u003c/figure\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cfigure\u003e\n      \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nstateDiagram-v2\n  [*] --\u003e Fetched: TomTom or Distance Matrix\n  Fetched --\u003e Classified: current vs free flow\n  Classified --\u003e Light: ratio 0.7 or more\n  Classified --\u003e Moderate: ratio 0.3 to 0.7\n  Classified --\u003e Heavy: ratio below 0.3\n  Light --\u003e Linked\n  Moderate --\u003e Linked\n  Heavy --\u003e Linked\n  Linked --\u003e Scored: read as disruption\n  Linked --\u003e Diffused: seen by NEAR_HOTEL neighbours\n  Diffused --\u003e Scored\n  Scored --\u003e Expired: older than 6 h\n  Expired --\u003e [*]: hourly DETACH DELETE\n\u003c/pre\u003e\n      \u003c/div\u003e\n      \u003cfigcaption\u003e\u003cb\u003eFigure 6.2.\u003c/b\u003e TrafficSignal lifecycle. Three results reversed along the way, and this timeline records them rather than smoothing them over.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003col class=\"timeline\"\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJun 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eWeighted retriever, personalisation, first harness\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eReplaced a single-MATCH context dump with intent parsing and composite scoring. Cleaned city aliases, duplicate hotels (49 → 40) and placeholder prices.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJul 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eBenchmark hardening\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eFound the gold was circular: Filter was graded by its own predicate. Rebuilt it as graded 2/1/0 with tolerance bands, where any hard failure disqualifies. Added pgvector keyword, dense and hybrid baselines, plus paired bootstrap, Wilcoxon and Holm tests. Built a balanced set of 60 queries, 10 per category.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJul–Aug 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eDiscrete-choice study, wave 1\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eNext.js app with real LiteAPI hotels and rooms: 11 tasks, 32 hotels, a hotel stage then a room stage. 268 consented and 241 were analysed, giving 2,232 hotel choices.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli class=\"neg\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e1 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eRL weight policy: null\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eA Dirichlet REINFORCE policy under nested CV scored 0.7991 ± 0.0074 against 0.8016 for the hand-tuned rules. An earlier positive result came from model selection on the test fold.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e2 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eScale fix and price slice\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eServing switched to percentile components. Added a price-direction slot and a 14-query ranked price slice, so the economic weight is observable at all.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli class=\"neg\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e13 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eWeight audit: wave 1 does not identify weights\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003e99.4% of choices were in the top 3 positions. With correct position controls, the components add nothing (χ²₅ = 8.7, p = .12). Wave 2 was redesigned with randomised order and gated identification.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e14 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eFull re-run, robustness, journal draft\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eFrozen intents for bit-identical runs. Added ablations, a 1,000-vector weight simplex, tolerance sensitivity, latency and a deployment audit. Drafted an 18-page Elsevier article in \u003ccode\u003epaper/\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n  \u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §8 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"evaluation\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§8\u003c/span\u003eEvaluation design\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nflowchart LR\n  subgraph R1[\"Route 1 - rule-based graded gold\"]\n    Q1[\"60 NL queries, 6 categories\"] --\u003e P1[\"frozen QueryIntent cache\"]\n    Q2[\"14 ranked price queries\"] --\u003e P1\n    P1 --\u003e S1[\"10 systems rank 58 hotels\"]\n    G1[\"gold.py: pass 2, band 1, fail 0, min over constraints\"] --\u003e M1\n    S1 --\u003e M1[\"P@10, R@10, graded nDCG@10, MRR\"]\n    M1 --\u003e T1[\"paired bootstrap CI, Wilcoxon, Holm\"]\n  end\n  subgraph R2[\"Route 2 - human choices\"]\n    C1[\"wave-1 bookings, 11 anchored tasks\"] --\u003e SPL[\"split by participant\"]\n    SPL --\u003e FT[\"fit weights on train people\"]\n    SPL --\u003e TE[\"score held-out people\"]\n    FT --\u003e TE\n    TE --\u003e M2[\"per-choice nDCG@10, MRR, mean rank\"]\n  end\n  T1 --\u003e REP[\"report both; neither supersedes\"]\n  M2 --\u003e REP\n\u003c/pre\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cfigcaption\u003e\u003cb\u003eFigure 8.1.\u003c/b\u003e Two independent measurement routes. \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"callout key\"\u003e\n    \u003ch4\u003eWhat the robustness evidence says\u003c/h4\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eThe contribution is \u003cstrong\u003efeasibility-first retrieval over graph-held travel-time features\u003c/strong\u003e: a hard-constraint gate followed by any reasonable blend of percentile components. It is not a particular weight vector, and on this snapshot it is not live disruption. The disruption signal cannot matter yet: pool disruption scores range 0.849–1.000 (sd 0.047), the largest route delay is 0.2 min, and no query is graded on delay.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §11 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"weights\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§11\u003c/span\u003eWeight-elicitation study\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eThe aim was to replace hand-set weights with weights estimated from human booking choices, using a discrete-choice experiment and a conditional logit model. Wave 1 collected 2,232 hotel choices from 241 analysed participants across 11 persona tasks (for example, \"Evening walker, Galle Face\"). A second stage, choosing a room within the chosen hotel, estimated willingness to pay.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"tablewrap\"\u003e\n \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"callout caveat\"\u003e\n    \u003ch4\u003eFinding: wave 1 measures list position, not preference\u003c/h4\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eParticipants picked from the top three of a list sorted by one of the very components being estimated, and 99.9% opened only one hotel. A −log(rank) control predicts a long tail that does not exist, so the components absorb position and look significant. With rank dummies, adding components makes held-out perplexity slightly \u003cem\u003eworse\u003c/em\u003e (3.024 → 3.027). The signal that does exist is in the \u003cstrong\u003esort choice\u003c/strong\u003e: 86.8% used the sort control, and on the cost-first task t3, 51% of those who switched chose price low-to-high. Stage 2 is valid within a hotel (β\u003csub\u003eprice\u003c/sub\u003e −1.976 per 10,000 LKR; breakfast worth ≈ 2,989 LKR), but it cannot be bridged to hotel-level weights, because the nested-logit λ = −0.036 falls outside (0, 1].\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003ch3\u003eWave 2: implemented, not yet fielded\u003c/h3\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv href=\"#next\"\u003e\u003cspan\u003e§13\u003c/span\u003eNext steps\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n  \u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003c/nav\u003e\n\n\u003cmain\u003e\n\u003cheader class=\"top\"\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"eyebrow\"\u003eProject review for supervisor · BSc research, index 28381\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003ch1\u003eALMA-GraphRAG: feasibility-aware hotel retrieval over a live knowledge graph\u003c/h1\u003e\n  \u003cp class=\"lede\"\u003eA graph-backed recommender for Colombo hotels that ranks by five feasibility dimensions instead of a single sort key, evaluated against nine baselines. The weights were also tested against a discrete-choice study with 241 analysed participants.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"meta\"\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eDilshan Sanjeewa Deshapriya\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eSupervisor: Dr Rasika Ranaweera\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eSnapshot: 15 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan\u003eBranch: sanjeewa/add-data-gather-machanisume\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/header\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §0 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"summary\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§0\u003c/span\u003eThe project in five minutes\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eHotel search normally filters on price or rating and sorts by one attribute. ALMA-GraphRAG stores Colombo hotels in a Neo4j property graph, together with their travel-time edges, traffic signals, news events and amenities. A natural-language question becomes a structured intent. The retriever first removes hotels that fail a hard constraint, then ranks the rest by a weighted sum of five percentile-normalised components: \u003cem\u003espatial, accessibility, facility, economic\u003c/em\u003e and \u003cem\u003edisruption\u003c/em\u003e. Every result carries its per-component breakdown, and an LLM loop (corrective RAG) turns the ranked list into an answer.\u003c/p\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eEvery number on this page comes from the canonical result files regenerated on 14 September 2026. The test suite passes \u003cspan class=\"mono\"\u003e364/364\u003c/span\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"stats\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e0.828\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003enDCG@10, WeightedGraphRAG, 60 queries\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e+0.252\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003eover Filter (OTA-style search), 95% CI [0.175, 0.328], p\u003csub\u003eHolm\u003c/sub\u003e \u0026lt; 0.001\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e−0.248\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003enDCG lost when the feasibility filter is removed: the main driver of the gain\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\u003cb\u003e2,232\u003c/b\u003e\u003cspan\u003ehuman choices used to estimate weight profiles; the 13 Sep audit found list position, not preference, drives them\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"verdict\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n      \u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan class=\"dot\" style=\"background:var(--good)\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003eHolds up\u003c/h4\u003e\n      \u003cul\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eBeats all seven non-circular baselines, significant after Holm correction\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eWins at all six gold-tolerance scales\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eAll 1,000 random weight vectors beat Filter, so the result doesn\u0027t depend on a lucky weight choice\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eRankings are explainable: every score decomposes into components\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003ePersonalisation demo: same F1 event, disjoint top-5 lists (0 overlap)\u003c/li\u003e\n      \u003c/ul\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n      \u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan class=\"dot\" style=\"background:var(--caveat)\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003eHolds with caveats\u003c/h4\u003e\n      \u003cul\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eGold is rule-based (graded, tolerance bands), not human-labelled\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eOne city, 58 hotels: a case study, not a benchmark\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eAn LTR model given the gold\u0027s own predicates scores 0.962, which shows how learnable the rule gold is\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003e41% of hotels have no price\u003c/li\u003e\n      \u003c/ul\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cdiv\u003e\n      \u003ch4\u003e\u003cspan class=\"dot\" style=\"background:var(--bad)\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003eNot supported yet\u003c/h4\u003e\n      \u003cul\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003e\"Disruption-aware\": removing disruption or diffusion changes 0 of 60 top-10 sets\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eHuman weights were estimated and shipped as profiles, but a later audit shows they mostly reflect list position (§11)\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eRL weight policy: trained, null result\u003c/li\u003e\n        \u003cli\u003eCorrective-RAG answer quality: never measured\u003c/li\u003e\n      \u003c/ul\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §1 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"questions\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§1\u003c/span\u003eProblem and research questions\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eA traveller\u0027s real question is not \"which hotel is cheapest\" but \"which hotel can I realistically get to, given where I need to be, what is happening in the city, and what I care about\". The proposition is to treat that as \u003cstrong\u003efeasibility-first, multi-criteria retrieval over a graph whose edges carry live, expiring signals\u003c/strong\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"tablewrap\"\u003e\n    \u003ctable\u003e\n      \u003cthead\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003cth\u003eRQ\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth\u003eQuestion\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth\u003eEvidence\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth\u003eStatus\u003c/th\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003c/thead\u003e\n      \u003ctbody\u003e\n        \u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd class=\"mono\"\u003eRQ1\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eCan a property graph with live edges support feasibility-aware retrieval?\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eGraph, pipelines, retriever built (§2–§5)\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003cspan class=\"chip good\"\u003ebuilt\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\n        \u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd class=\"mono\"\u003eRQ2\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eDoes weighted multi-criteria retrieval beat lexical, dense, hybrid and re-ranking baselines?\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003e0.828 vs best non-circular baseline 0.576 (§9)\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003e\u003cspan class=\"chip \u003c/table\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §2 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"architecture\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§2\u003c/span\u003eEnd-to-end architecture\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eFour external providers feed scheduled ingestion jobs, which write to Neo4j with idempotent \u003ccode\u003eMERGE\u003c/code\u003e statements. Hotel text is mirrored into pgvector for the lexical and dense baselines. At query time, the parser, retriever and corrective-RAG loop read the graph. A separate Next.js study app collects human choices; Python fitters turn those choices into candidate weight profiles.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nflowchart LR\n  subgraph SRC[\"External providers\"]\n    GP[\"Google Places\"]\n    LA[\"LiteAPI rates, rooms, facilities\"]\n    NW[\"NewsAPI, GNews, RSS\"]\n    GD[\"Google Distance Matrix\"]\n    TT[\"TomTom flow and incidents\"]\n  end\n  subgraph ING[\"Ingestion - query\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nsequenceDiagram\n  autonumber\n  actor U as Traveller\n  participant API as FastAPI /query\n  participant RC as Redis\n  participant QP as query_parser\n  participant WR as WeightedRetriever\n  participant NEO as Neo4j\n  participant LLM as LLM\n  U-\u003e\u003eAPI: question, city\n  API-\u003e\u003eRC: get md5(city, question)\n  alt cache hit\n    RC--\u003e\u003eAPI: cached answer\n    API--\u003e\u003eU: answer\n  else cache miss\n    API-\u003e\u003eQP: parse_query\n    QP-\u003e\u003eQP: regex slots (deterministic)\n    QP-\u003e\u003eLLM: slot-fill proximity, sort, price direction\n    LLM--\u003e\u003eQP: slots (neutral values never override regex)\n    QP--\u003e\u003eWR: QueryIntent\n    WR-\u003e\u003eNEO: candidate query with 2-hop NEAR_HOTEL diffusion\n    NEO--\u003e\u003eWR: 58 candidates + collected attributes\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: base profile + intent ladder, renormalise\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: feasibility filter (unknown value fails)\n    WR-\u003e\u003eWR: percentile components, weighted sum, top 10\n    WR--\u003e\u003eAPI: ranked hotels + breakdown\n    API-\u003e\u003eLLM: grade relevance of context\n    opt score below 0.6 and retries left\n      API-\u003e\u003eLLM: rewrite question\n      API-\u003e\u003eWR: retrieve again\n      API-\u003e\u003eLLM: re-grade\n    end\n    API-\u003e\u003eLLM: generate grounded answer\n    API-\u003e\u003eRC: set, TTL 900 s\n    API--\u003e\u003eU: answer + ranked hotels\n  end\n\u003c/pre\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cfigcaption\u003e\u003cb\u003eFigure 5.1.\u003c/b\u003e Request path for \u003ccode\u003ePOST /query\u003c/code\u003e. The LLM never writes Cypher; it only fills a fixed \u003ccode\u003eQueryIntent\u003c/code\u003e schema that binds to a parameterised query. This keeps the data path injection-safe. For reproducibility, the evaluation uses frozen intent caches.\u003c/figcaption\u003e\n  \u003c/figure\u003e\n\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n      \u003csvg measured. The accuracy gain over OTA-style search comes mostly from the filter that runs \u003cem\u003ebefore\u003c/em\u003e scoring. The disruption term is drawn dashed because it has no measurable effect on the current data snapshot.\u003c/figcaption\u003e\n  \u003c/figure\u003e\n\n  \u003ch3\u003eScoring model\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"formula\"\u003escore(h) = w_spa·spatial + w_acc·accessibility + w_fac·facility + w_eco·economic + w_dis·disruption    Σw = 1\nspatial       = percentile, lower distance_km better          (flipped if proximity = far)\naccessibility = percentile, lower travel_time_traffic_min better\nfacility      = 0.40·amenity match + 0.20·attraction match + 0.20·pct(rating) + 0.20·pct(star)\neconomic      = percentile, lower price better; unknown price → 0.5   (flipped if price = high)\ndisruption    = own route signal (0.7) blended with NEAR_HOTEL neighbourhood exposure (0.3)\u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"tablewrap\"\u003e\n      \u003ctable\u003e\n Three results reversed along the way, and this timeline records them rather than smoothing them over.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003col class=\"timeline\"\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJun 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eWeighted retriever, personalisation, first harness\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eReplaced a single-MATCH context dump with intent parsing and composite scoring. Cleaned city aliases, duplicate hotels (49 → 40) and placeholder prices.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJul 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eBenchmark hardening\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eFound the gold was circular: Filter was graded by its own predicate. Rebuilt it as graded 2/1/0 with tolerance bands, where any hard failure disqualifies. Added pgvector keyword, dense and hybrid baselines, plus paired bootstrap, Wilcoxon and Holm tests. Built a balanced set of 60 queries, 10 per category.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003eJul–Aug 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eDiscrete-choice study, wave 1\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eNext.js app with real LiteAPI hotels and rooms: 11 tasks, 32 hotels, a hotel stage then a room stage. 268 consented and 241 were analysed, giving 2,232 hotel choices.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli class=\"neg\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e1 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eRL weight policy: null\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eA Dirichlet REINFORCE policy under nested CV scored 0.7991 ± 0.0074 against 0.8016 for the hand-tuned rules. An earlier positive result came from model selection on the test fold.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e2 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eScale fix and price slice\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eServing switched to percentile components. Added a price-direction slot and a 14-query ranked price slice, so the economic weight is observable at all.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli class=\"neg\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e13 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eWeight audit: wave 1 does not identify weights\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003e99.4% of choices were in the top 3 positions. With correct position controls, the components add nothing (χ²₅ = 8.7, p = .12). Wave 2 was redesigned with randomised order and gated identification.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n    \u003cli\u003e\u003cspan class=\"when\"\u003e14 Sep 2026\u003c/span\u003e\u003cb\u003eFull re-run, robustness, journal draft\u003c/b\u003e\u003cp\u003eFrozen intents for bit-identical runs. Added ablations, a 1,000-vector weight simplex, tolerance sensitivity, latency and a deployment audit. Drafted an 18-page Elsevier article in \u003ccode\u003epaper/\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n  \u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §8 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"evaluation\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§8\u003c/span\u003eEvaluation design\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cfigure\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"diagram\"\u003e\n\u003cpre class=\"mermaid\"\u003e\nflowchart LR\n  subgraph R1[\"Route 1 - rule-based graded gold\"]\n    Q1[\"60 NL queries, 6 categories\"] --\u003e P1[\"frozen QueryIntent cache\"]\n    Q2[\"14 ranked price queries\"] --\u003e P1\n    P1 --\u003e S1[\"10 systems rank 58 hotels\"]\n    G1[\"gold.py: pass 2, band 1, fail 0, min over constraints\"] --\u003e M1\n    S1 --\u003e M1[\"P@10, R@10, graded nDCG@10, MRR\"]\n    M1 --\u003e T1[\"paired bootstrap CI, Wilcoxon, Holm\"]\n  end\n  subgraph R2[\"Route 2 - human choices\"]\n    C1[\"wave-1 bookings, 11 anchored tasks\"] --\u003e SPL[\"split by participant\"]\n    SPL --\u003e FT[\"fit weights on train people\"]\n    SPL --\u003e TE[\"score held-out people\"]\n    FT --\u003e TE\n    TE --\u003e M2[\"per-choice nDCG@10, MRR, mean rank\"]\n  end\n  T1 --\u003e REP[\"report both; neither supersedes\"]\n  M2 --\u003e REP\n\u003c/pre\u003e\n    \u003c/div\u003e\n    \u003cfigcaption\u003e\u003cb\u003eFigure 8.1.\u003c/b\u003e Two independent measurement routes. Route 1 tests query understanding and constraint satisfaction. Route 2 tests agreement with what people chose, but inherits the wave-1 position bias described in §11.\u003c/figcaption\u003e\n  \u003c/figure\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n to read the profile rows\u003c/h4\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eOn the main set, no profile differs significantly from handset after Holm correction. The main set only separates profiles on amenity, accessibility and disruption queries (30 of 60 top-10 sets change). The human route has 95 clean-cohort participants, 28 held out with 280 choices, and it favours location-heavy profiles. But those participants chose from lists \u003cem\u003esorted by the same components\u003c/em\u003e, so part of that agreement is top-of-list clicking, not preference (§11).\u003c/p\u003e\n \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"callout key\"\u003e\n    \u003ch4\u003eWhat the robustness evidence says\u003c/h4\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eThe contribution is \u003cstrong\u003efeasibility-first retrieval over graph-held travel-time features\u003c/strong\u003e: a hard-constraint gate followed by any reasonable blend of percentile components. It is not a particular weight vector, and on this snapshot it is not live disruption. The disruption signal cannot matter yet: pool disruption scores range 0.849–1.000 (sd 0.047), the largest route delay is 0.2 min, and no query is graded on delay.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/section\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- §11 --\u003e\n\u003csection id=\"weights\"\u003e\n  \u003ch2\u003e\u003cspan class=\"sec\"\u003e§11\u003c/span\u003eWeight-elicitation study\u003c/h2\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"prose\"\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eThe aim was to replace hand-set weights with weights estimated from human booking choices, using a discrete-choice experiment and a conditional logit model. Wave 1 collected 2,232 hotel choices from 241 analysed participants across 11 persona tasks (for example, \"Evening walker, Galle Face\"). A second stage, choosing a room within the chosen hotel, estimated willingness to pay.\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv class=\"tablewrap\"\u003e\n      \u003ctable\u003e\n        \u003cthead\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003cth\u003eSort in use\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth class=\"n\"\u003eChoices\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth class=\"n\"\u003ePos 1\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth class=\"n\"\u003ePos 2\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth class=\"n\"\u003ePos 3\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth class=\"n\"\u003eBelow\u003c/th\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003c/thead\u003e\n        \u003ctbody\u003e\n          \u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003eDistance\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd class=\"n\"\u003e986\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd class=\"n\"\u003e45.7%\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd \u003c/div\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003cdiv class=\"callout caveat\"\u003e\n    \u003ch4\u003eFinding: wave 1 measures list position, not preference\u003c/h4\u003e\n    \u003cp\u003eParticipants picked from the top three of a list sorted by one of the very components being estimated, and 99.9% opened only one hotel. A −log(rank) control predicts a long tail that does not exist, so the components absorb position and look significant. With rank dummies, adding components makes held-out perplexity slightly \u003cem\u003eworse\u003c/em\u003e (3.024 → 3.027). The signal that does exist is in the \u003cstrong\u003esort choice\u003c/strong\u003e: 86.8% used the sort control, and on the cost-first task t3, 51% of those who switched chose price low-to-high. Stage 2 is valid within a hotel (β\u003csub\u003eprice\u003c/sub\u003e −1.976 per 10,000 LKR; breakfast worth ≈ 2,989 LKR), but it cannot be bridged to hotel-level weights, because the nested-logit λ = −0.036 falls outside (0, 1].\u003c/p\u003e\n  \u003c/div\u003e\n\n  \u003ch3\u003eWave 2: implemented, not yet fielded\u003c/h3\u003e\n  \u003cdiv class=\"grid2\"\u003e\n    \u003cdiv
